LP: maximize 1^T g subject to A g ≤ M, g ≥ 0, where A[k,i] = f[i] if 0≤k-i≤n-1. A banded (Toeplitz). We can solve this LP more efficiently using its dual or active set.

Dual: minimize M ∑_k y_k subject to A^T y ≥ 1, y ≥0. A^T y at i = ∑_k A[k,i] y_k = ∑_{j=0}^{2n-2} f[i+j?]. More precisely ∑_{k: i∈[k-n+1,k]} f[k-i] y_k = ∑_{j=-(n-1)}^{n-1} f[i+j?] y_{i+j?}. This is a convolution of y with f. Dual has 2n-1 variables y, n constraints. Could be solved with an interior point? But still.

Smooth gradient can be applied to n=6733 and n=2048. It may improve power sequence without LP cost. Let's implement a robust smooth optimizer:

```
def smooth_optimize(x, n, time_budget, seed, initial_beta=50, max_beta=2000):
   best = x; best_score = score(x)
   beta = initial_beta; eta=0.05; stall=0
   while time left:
      conv = fft_conv(x)
      M=conv.max()
      shifted = beta*(conv - M)
      w = exp(shifted); w /= sum
      grad = 2*corr(w,x)
      grad -= grad.mean()
      # maybe scale grad by (best_score * eta?) no.
      for step in [eta, eta*0.5, eta*2] maybe:
          y = x - step*grad
          y = clip; normalize
          s=score(y)
          choose best step
      if improved: ... beta slowly increase; if many steps increase eta
      else: perturb x; reset eta
```

```
import numpy as np, time, sys
from scipy.optimize import linprog
from scipy import sparse

def next_pow2(n): ...
def normalize(x): s=x.sum(); if s<...; return np.maximum(x,0)/s
def fft_convolve(x,n): L=1; while L<2*n-1: L*=2; F=np.fft.rfft(x,L); conv=np.fft.irfft(F*F,L)[:2*n-1]; return conv
def score_fft(x,n): conv=fft_convolve(x,n); M=conv.max(); s=x.sum(); if s<1e-12 return inf; return 2*n*M/(s*s)
def exact_score(x): return evaluate_sequence([float(v) for v in x])
```

def make_power(n, delta=1.0, tilt=0.0, seed=None):
    i=np.arange(n)+delta
    x=i**-0.5
    if tilt!=0: x *= (1 + tilt*(np.arange(n)/n -0.5)); x=np.clip(x,0,None)
    rng... noise
    return normalize(x)

LP function:
```
def run_sota(x0, n, time_budget, seed, verbose):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    x = normalize(x0.copy())
    current = score_fft(x,n)
    best = x.copy(); best_score=current
    # preallocate? dense A if n<=2048:
    if n <= 2048:
       A = np.zeros((2*n-1,n))
       use_dense=True
    else:
       # build sparse pattern maybe via diags each iter? or precompute row/col/data fill
       use_dense=False
    diag_lens = np.arange(n,0,-1)
    t = 0.25
    no_improve=0
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
       conv=fft_convolve(x,n); M=conv.max()
       # build A
       if use_dense:
          # Need reset A to zeros? Only fill upper diagonals; lower should be zero. We fill all upper diagonals each iter; entries where x[j]=0 still assigned zero. The full upper triangle overwritten with new x[j]. Lower zeros remain from init. But if previous had nonzero lower? We never fill lower, so zeros.
          for j in range(n): A[j:j+n-j, :n-j] = x[j]
          A_ub=A
       else:
          diags=[]
          for j in range(n): diags.append(x[j]*np.ones(n-j))
          A_ub=sparse.diags(diags, offsets=np.arange(n), shape=(2*n-1,n), format='csc')
       b = M * np.ones(2*n-1)
       c = -np.ones(n)
       res = linprog(c, A_ub=A_ub, b_ub=b, bounds=[(0,None)]*n, method='highs')
       if not res.success or res.x is None: perturb...
       g=res.x
       g=np.maximum(g,0); sg=g.sum()
       if sg <= 0: perturb
       g/=sg
       # try t values
       best_next = None
       for step in [1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125,0.0625]:
           y = (1-step)*x + step*g
           y=normalize(y)
           sc = score_fft(y,n)
           if sc < best_score: update best_next
       if best_next is not None:
           x=best_next; best_score=...; no_improve=0
           if best_score < global_best: update global
           # adjust t? use successful step? 
       else:
           no_improve+=1
           if no_improve > 3:
              # perturb x: mix with random or g reverse, etc.
              if no_improve>10: x = normalize(0.9*x + 0.1*make_random_power(n,rng))
              else: x=normalize(x + 0.05*rng.standard_normal(n)); clip
              no_improve=0
       # if score very close target, can stop? But keep for improvement.
```

Now, smooth gradient implementation details for large n without LP:

```
def fft_corr(w,x,n,Lcorr=None):
   # need G[i]=sum_m x[m]*w[m+i]? Wait gradient = 2*sum_k weights[k]*x[k-i]; we derived G=ifft(FFT(w)*conj(FFT(x)))[:n] with w length 2n-1, x length n, L >= 3n-2.
   Lc=1; while Lc < (2*n-1)+n-1: Lc*=2
   W=np.fft.rfft(w,Lc); X=np.fft.rfft(x,Lc); G=np.fft.irfft(W*np.conj(X),Lc)[:n]
   return 2*G
```
Need verify formula with a small test in code? We can't run, but can reason. Let's test n=2, x=[a,b], w=[w0,w1,w2] (conv length3). Desired grad for x0: 2*(w0*x0? ∂c0/∂x0=2x0 but weights w0; ∂c1/∂x0=2x1 weight w1; ∂c2/∂x0? c2=b², independent x0? Actually c2=x1², derivative 0. So grad0=2(w0*x0 + w1*x1). grad1=2(w1*x0 + w2*x1). Our formula G[i]=∑_m x[m] w[m+i]? For i=0: m0 x0 w0 + m1 x1 w1 -> yes. i=1: m0 x0 w1 + m1 x1 w2 -> yes. Good.
FFT: if L>= len(w)+len(x)-1=3n-2? Here 5? formula ifft(FFT(w)*conj(FFT(x))) gives circular cross-correlation? Standard circular cross-correlation: ifft(conj(FFT(a))*FFT(b))[k] = ∑_j a[j] b[(j-k) mod L]. Here W*conj(X)=FFT(w)*conj(FFT(x)) = conj(conj(W)*X)? Using formula with a=x,b=w: ifft(conj(X)*W)[k]=∑ x[j] w[(j-k) mod L]. That's x[j]w[j-k]. We want x[j]w[j+i] for k? Set k=-i mod L. Ifft at L-i gives ∑ x[j]w[j+ i]. So we can take negative lags. Let's use property: cross-correlation (x ⊛ w)[i] = ∑_j x[j] conj(w[j+i]) = ifft(conj(FFT(x)) * FFT(w))[i]? Derive: DFT of x̃? Let ifft(conj(X)*W)[i] = 1/L∑_k conj(X_k) W_k e^{ik i} =∑_j x[j] w? Sum=1 if i+j-l=0 mod L => l=i+j. So result ∑_j x_j w_{j+i}. Great! So G[i]=ifft(conj(FFT(x)) * FFT(w))[i]. Thus use `np.fft.irfft(np.conj(X) * W, Lc)[:n]`, not W*conj(X) but multiplication commutative; same: W*conj(X). Yes product same. Earlier I wrote W*np.conj(X), same. def smooth_optimize(x,n,budget,seed, beta_factor=None):

   eta_rel=0.5

      conv=fft_convolve(x,n); M=conv.max()
      beta = current_beta * n? Let current_beta as multiple of n/M? Set `temperature = M * temperature_factor`; better: weights = exp((conv - M)/scale). Choose scale = M/tau, with tau controls active width. tau=10 initially (top e^10). So shifted = tau*(conv-M)/M. Then weights.
      tau increase from 5 to 500.

Detailed sota loop:
```
def sota_lp_optimize(x0, n, deadline, seed=0, target=1.5027, dense_limit=2560, max_lp_calls=None):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    x = np.asarray(x0, float); x=norm_nonneg(x)
    current = fft_score(x,n)
    best = x.copy(); best_score = current
    global_best = (best,best_score)
    use_dense = n <= dense_limit
    if use_dense:
       A = np.zeros((2*n-1,n), float)
    else:
       A = None
    t_choices = (1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125,0.0625,0.03125)
    stall = 0
    lp_count=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       conv = fft_conv(x,n); M = conv.max()
       if not np.isfinite(M) or M<=0: break
       f_scaled = n * x
       M_scaled = n * M
       try:
          if use_dense:
             for j in range(n):
                 A[j:j+n-j, :n-j] = f_scaled[j]
             A_ub = A
          else:
             diags = [f_scaled[j] * np.ones(n-j) for j in range(n)]
             A_ub = sparse.diags(diags, offsets=np.arange(n), shape=(2*n-1,n), format='csc')
          b = M_scaled * np.ones(2*n-1)
          res = linprog(-np.ones(n), A_ub=A_ub, b_ub=b, bounds=(0,None), method='highs')
          if not res.success:
             raise RuntimeError
          g = np.asarray(res.x, float)
          g = np.clip(g,0,None)
          sg = g.sum()
          if sg <= 0: raise RuntimeError
          g /= sg
       except Exception:
          # perturb and continue
          x = norm_nonneg(x + 0.05*rng.standard_normal(n))
          continue
       lp_count += 1
       best_y = None; best_y_score = current
       for t in t_choices:
           y = (1.0-t)*x + t*g
           y = norm_nonneg(y)
           sc = fft_score(y,n)
           if sc < best_y_score:
               best_y_score=sc; best_y=y
       if best_y is not None and best_y_score < current:
           x = best_y; current = best_y_score
           if current < best_score:
               best_score = current; best = x.copy()
               # if best_score < target maybe still continue but can track
           stall = 0
           if lp_count % 50 == 0: print(...)
       else:
           stall += 1
           if stall >= 3:
               # try mixing with a fresh direction based on current g or random
               mix = norm_nonneg(0.8*x + 0.2*g)
               sc = fft_score(mix,n)
               if sc < current:
                   x=mix; current=sc; update
               else:
                   x = norm_nonneg(0.9*x + 0.1*make_power(n, rng.uniform(0.1,2.0), rng.uniform(-0.3,0.3), noise=0.05, seed=rng))
               stall=0
           if stall >= 20:
               # reset to a different random start but keep best? maybe restart local from best with perturbation
               x = norm_nonneg(0.95*best + 0.05*rng.standard_normal(n))
               current = fft_score(x,n)
               stall=0
       # optional if best_score < target*0.999? continue
    return best, best_score
```

Time check: If LP fails repeatedly, may loop; add stall reset.

Need `make_power` signature without closure. It can take seed Optional. But for random in sota, pass `rng`? Helper can't close. Let's define:
```
def make_power(n, delta=1.0, tilt=0.0, noise=0.0, rng=None):
   i = np.arange(n)
   x = np.power(i + max(delta,1e-6), -0.5)
   if tilt !=0: x *= (1 + tilt*(i/n -0.5)); x=np.maximum(x,0)
   if noise>0 and rng is not None: x *= (1 + noise*rng.standard_normal(n)); x=np.maximum(x,0)
   return norm_nonneg(x)
```
For random start with `rng`, pass rng.

def smooth_optimize(x0,n,deadline,seed=0):
   rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
   x=norm_nonneg(x0)
   best=x; best_score=fft_score(x,n)
   tau=5.0
   eta=0.5
   stall=0
   L1=next_pow2(2*n-1); L2=next_pow2(3*n-2)
   while time.monotonic()<deadline:
       conv=fft_conv_with_L(x,n,L1); M=conv.max()
       shifted = tau*(conv-M)/M
       w=np.exp(shifted); w/=w.sum()
       W=np.fft.rfft(w,L2); X=np.fft.rfft(x,L2)
       G=np.fft.irfft(W*np.conj(X),L2)[:n]
       grad=2*G
       # subtract mean projected? Use grad_proj = grad - np.mean(grad) maybe to preserve sum before normalize
       grad_proj = grad - np.mean(grad)
       base_scale = max(np.max(np.abs(grad_proj)), 1e-300)
       for rel in [eta, eta*0.5, eta*2, 0.1,0.05]:
           y = x - (rel/base_scale)*grad_proj
           y=np.clip(y,0,None); y=norm_nonneg(y)
           sc=fft_score(y,n,L1)   # But fft_score recomputes L; add L param.
       if improve: ... tau*=1.03; eta*=1.02
       else: stall++; if stall>5: x=norm_nonneg(0.9*x+0.1*rng.standard_normal(n)); tau=max(2,tau*0.7); eta*=0.7; stall=0
   return best,best_score
```
But `base_scale` may be tiny; rel/base_scale*grad gives changes O(rel). This is like relative step in x (if x ~1, too large; if x~1/n, still rel absolute, might blow zeros). Better step = rel * x? If rel=0.1, changes 10%. Good. We can use multiplicative log-space. After normalize.

Let's draft code mentally.

```
import numpy as np
import time
from scipy.optimize import linprog
from scipy import sparse

PRINT = True

def next_pow2(m):
    p=1
    while p < m: p <<=1
    return p

def norm_nonneg(x):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.where(np.isfinite(x), x, 0.0)
    x = np.maximum(x, 0.0)
    s = x.sum()
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return np.ones_like(x, dtype=np.float64)/max(1,x.size)
    return x/s
```

`fft_conv` with optional L:
```
def fft_convolve(x, n, L=None):
    if L is None: L=next_pow2(2*n-1)
    F=np.fft.rfft(x, L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(F*F, L)
    return c[:2*n-1]
```

`fft_score`:
```
def fft_score(x,n,L=None):
   conv=fft_convolve(x,n,L)
   M=conv.max() if conv.size else 0
   s=x.sum()
   if s < 1e-12: return np.inf
   return float(2.0*n*M/(s*s))
```

`make_envelope`:
```
def make_envelope(n, alpha=0.5, delta=1.0, tilt=0.0, mod_a=0.0, mod_p=1.0, noise=0.0, rng=None, uniform_mix=0.0):
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    base = np.power(i + max(float(delta), 1e-6), -float(alpha))
    if tilt != 0.0:
       base *= (1.0 + float(tilt)*(i/(n-1) if n>1 else 0.0) - 0.5?) Wait factor should center: 1 + tilt*(i/n - 0.5)
    if mod_a != 0.0:
       z = i / max(n,1)
       base *= (1.0 + float(mod_a)*np.power(z, float(mod_p)))
    if uniform_mix:
       base += float(uniform_mix)
    if noise and rng is not None:
       base *= (1.0 + float(noise)*rng.standard_normal(n))
    return norm_nonneg(base)
```

Let's implement `build_affine_lp_matrix(n)`:
```
def build_affine_lp_matrix(n):
    counts = np.arange(n, 0, -1, dtype=np.int64)
    indptr = np.empty(n+1, dtype=np.int32)
    indptr[0]=0
    indptr[1:] = np.cumsum(counts, dtype=np.int32)
    nnz = int(indptr[-1])
    indices = np.empty(nnz, dtype=np.int32)
    for i in range(n):
        st=indptr[i]; en=indptr[i+1]
        # if n-i length
        indices[st:en] = np.arange(i, i + (n - i), dtype=np.int32)
    data = np.zeros(nnz, dtype=np.float64)
    A = sparse.csc_matrix((data, indices, indptr), shape=(2*n-1, n))
    return A, indptr
```
For n large, `np.arange` in loop; okay. Could vectorize with `np.repeat`? Not needed.

        data[st:en] = f_scaled[:length]

m_factor = 1.0
stall=0
while time left:
   conv=fft_convolve; M=max
   fill data
   b = m_factor * (n*M)
   res=linprog(...)

      m_factor = max(0.7, m_factor*0.9); stall++...
   else get g
   try t values with m_factor? The direction g solves constraints at m_factor M. If m_factor<1, better.
   if improved: m_factor = 1.0 (reset); stall=0

   if stall>20: perturb x maybe; m_factor=1

def add_to_pool(pool, x, score, max_items=8):
    if x is None or score is not np.isfinite(score): return
    # avoid too large n? if len(x)>20000 maybe skip exact pool? But keep best seq separately.
    for item in pool:
        if len(item[0]) == len(x) and np.allclose(item[0], x, rtol=1e-5, atol=1e-8): return
    pool.append((score, x.copy()))
    pool.sort(key=lambda z: z[0]) # lambda not allowed? The rule says no lambda functions. Use `pool.sort(key=lambda ...)` would violate. We can avoid sorting or use manual insertion.
```

```
def prune_pool(pool, max_items):
   while len(pool)>max_items:
      worst_idx=0; worst_val=-np.inf
      for idx,(sc,x) in enumerate(pool):
          if sc > worst_val: worst_val=sc; worst_idx=idx
      pool.pop(worst_idx)
```

def propose_candidate(seed=1, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 25.0)
    best_seq = None
    best_fft = np.inf
    pool=[]
    # helper local? can't. Use loops.
    def? no.
    # quick eval of some inits? We can call quick_best_init for each n and update.
    ns = [128,256,512,1024,2048,4096]
    for n in ns:
        x0 = quick_best_init(n, seed+ n)
        sc = fft_score(x0,n)
        if sc < best_fft: best_fft=sc; best_seq=x0.copy(); pool...
    # maybe add parent if available
    try:
       p=np.asarray(height_sequence_1,float)
       if p.size>0 and p.size<=10000:
          x0=norm_nonneg(p); sc=fft_score(x0,p.size); update
    except: pass
    # main SOTA allocations
    # If best_fft maybe inf, fallback
    allocations = [(512, 90), (1024, 300), (2048, 1200), (4096, 600)]
    # scale allocations to remaining
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    total_alloc = sum(...)=2190; scale = remaining/total_alloc maybe min 1.
    for n, t_alloc in allocations:
       if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 60: break
       slice_deadline = min(deadline-30, time.monotonic() + t_alloc*scale)
       x0 = quick_best_init(n, seed+n)  # could reuse, but cheap
       local_best, local_sc = sota_lp_optimize(x0, n, slice_deadline, seed+123*n)
       if local_sc < best_fft: update
       add pool
    # If still time, smooth optimize best_seq
    if best_seq is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 15:
       # maybe smooth only if len<=8192
       if len(best_seq) <= 8192:
          local_best, local_sc = smooth_optimize(best_seq, len(best_seq), deadline-10, seed+777)
          update
    # Also try SOTA on parent length if time? Maybe include allocation (6733,300) if budget large and memory ok.
    # Final exact
    final_candidates = [(best_fft, best_seq)] + pool
    # do not duplicate exact too many.
    final_seq = best_seq
    final_exact = np.inf
    for sc, x in final_candidates:
       if x is None or len(x)>20000: continue
       exact = exact_score(x)
       if exact < final_exact:
           final_exact=exact; final_seq=x
    if final_seq is None:
       final_seq = norm_nonneg(np.ones(3)).tolist()
    # ensure list floats
    return [float(v) for v in np.clip(final_seq,0,1000)]
```

def smooth_optimize(x0,n,deadline,seed=0):
    if n < 2 or time.monotonic() > deadline: return x0, fft_score(x0,n)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    x=norm_nonneg(x0)
    best=x.copy(); best_score=fft_score(x,n)
    L1=next_pow2(2*n-1); L2=next_pow2(3*n-2)
    tau=5.0
    eta=0.2
    stall=0
    last_improve=time.monotonic()
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 1:
       conv=fft_convolve(x,n,L1); M=conv.max()
       if not finite: break
       shifted = tau*(conv - M)/M
       # for numerical, clip shifted to [-700,0]
       np.clip(shifted, -700, 0, out=shifted)  # but shifted may be -0
       w=np.exp(shifted); ws=w.sum(); if ws<=0: break
       w*=1/ws
       W=np.fft.rfft(w,L2); X=np.fft.rfft(x,L2)
       G=np.fft.irfft(W*np.conj(X),L2)[:n]
       grad=2.0*G
       grad -= np.mean(grad)
       scale = np.max(np.abs(grad))
       if scale ==0: break
       best_y=None; best_sc=best_score
       for rel in (eta, eta*0.5, eta*0.25, 0.05, 0.01):
           y = x * np.exp(-rel*grad/scale)
           # Could underflow/overflow if rel*grad/scale large; clip exponent
           # Better compute exponent = -rel*grad/scale, clip to [-5,5], then y=x*exp(exponent)
           # Do inside loop.
```

def fill_sota_data(A, indptr, f_scaled, n):
    d = A.data
    # d is 1D length nnz
    for i in range(n):
        length = n - i
        st = indptr[i]
        en = indptr[i+1]
        d[st:en] = f_scaled[:length]
```

In `add_candidate`, to prune without lambda:
```
def add_candidate(pool, x, score, max_items=8):
    if x is None or not np.isfinite(score): return
    # skip if score much worse?
    pool.append((score, np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).copy()))
    while len(pool) > max_items:
       worst = 0; worst_sc = -np.inf
       for i in range(len(pool)):
           if pool[i][0] > worst_sc:
               worst_sc = pool[i][0]; worst = i
       pool.pop(worst)
```

def sota_lp_optimize(x0, n, deadline, seed=0, verbose=True):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    x=norm_nonneg(x0)
    best=x.copy(); best_score=fft_score(x,n)
    L=next_pow2(2*n-1)
    try:
       A, indptr = build_sota_matrix(n)
    except Exception:
       return best,best_score
    m_factor=1.0
    stall=0
    best_step_t=0.25
    step_trials=(1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125,0.0625,0.03125)
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       conv=fft_convolve(x,n,L); M=conv.max()
       if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 0: break
       f_scaled = n*x
       M_scaled = n*M
       try:
          fill_sota_data(A, indptr, f_scaled, n)
       except Exception:
          # rebuild with new data? break
          break
       b = (m_factor*M_scaled)*np.ones(2*n-1, dtype=np.float64)
       # jitter
       if rng.random() < 0.2:
          c = -1.0 - 1e-7*rng.standard_normal(n)
       else:
          c = -np.ones(n)
       remaining = max(1.0, float(deadline) - time.monotonic())
       if n <= 1024: tl = min(30.0, remaining/2.0)
       elif n <= 2560: tl = min(60.0, remaining/2.0)
       else: tl = min(120.0, remaining/2.0)
       try:
          res = linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=(0.0, None), method='highs', options={'time_limit': max(2.0, tl)})
       except Exception:
          res = None
       if res is None or res.x is None or not np.isfinite(res.x).all():
          m_factor = max(0.7, m_factor*0.9)
          stall += 1
          if stall > 10:

             stall=0; m_factor=1.0
          continue
       g = np.array(res.x, dtype=np.float64); g=np.maximum(g,0); sg=g.sum()
       if sg <= 1e-12:
          m_factor=max(0.7, m_factor*0.9); stall+=1
          if stall>5: x=...; 
          continue
       g /= sg
       # line search
       current = best_score? Actually local current should be fft_score(x,n,L). We don't maintain current separate? Use current = fft_score(x,n,L) before LP or reuse. Let's set `current = fft_score(x,n,L)` after M? We have conv M; but score =2nM/s^2 (sum1) =2nM. Since x normalized sum1. But use function.
       current = fft_score(x,n,L)
       best_y=None; best_y_score=current
       for t in step_trials:
          y = (1.0 - t)*x + t*g
          y=norm_nonneg(y)
          sc=fft_score(y,n,L)
          if sc < best_y_score:

          x=best_y; current=best_y_score
          if current < best_score:
             best_score=current; best=x.copy()
             if verbose and best_score < 1.8: print...
          stall=0
          m_factor=1.0

             x = make_envelope(n, delta=float(rng.uniform(0.05,2.0)), tilt=float(rng.uniform(-0.5,0.5)), mod_a=float(rng.uniform(-0.3,0.3)), mod_p=1.0, noise=0.05, rng=rng)
             current=fft_score(x,n,L)
             stall=0; m_factor=1.0
    return best,best_score

Now, smooth_optimize: Need avoid `np.clip` with out on a scalar? okay.

```
def smooth_optimize(x0,n,deadline,seed=0,verbose=False):
   if n < 2: return x0, fft_score(x0,n)
   x=norm_nonneg(x0); best=x.copy(); best_score=fft_score(x,n)
   L1=next_pow2(2*n-1); L2=next_pow2(3*n-2)
   rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
   tau=5.0; eta=0.2; stall=0; bad=0
   while time.monotonic() < deadline:
      conv=fft_convolve(x,n,L1); M=conv.max()
      if not finite or M<=0: break
      shifted = tau*(conv - M)/M
      np.clip(shifted, -700.0, 0.0, out=shifted)
      w=np.exp(shifted); ws=w.sum()
      if ws <=0: break
      w /= ws
      W=np.fft.rfft(w,L2); X=np.fft.rfft(x,L2)
      G=np.fft.irfft(W*np.conj(X),L2)[:n]
      grad=2.0*G
      grad = grad - np.mean(grad)
      scale=np.max(np.abs(grad))
      if scale <= 1e-300: break
      gn = grad/scale
      best_y=None; best_sc=best_score
      for rel in (eta, eta*0.5, eta*0.25, 0.05, 0.01):
         expr = -rel*gn
         np.clip(expr, -4.0, 4.0, out=expr)
         y = x * np.exp(expr)
         y = norm_nonneg(y)
         sc = fft_score(y,n,L1)
         if sc < best_sc:
            best_sc=sc; best_y=y
      if best_y is not None:
         x=best_y; best_score=best_sc; stall=0; bad=0
         if best_score < 1.6 and verbose: print...
         tau = min(200.0, tau*1.03)
         eta = min(1.0, eta*1.05)
      else:
         stall +=1; bad +=1
         if stall > 8:
            x = norm_nonneg(0.9*x + 0.1*rng.standard_normal(n))
            tau = max(2.0, tau*0.8)
            eta = max(0.05, eta*0.7)
            stall=0; bad=0
         if bad > 20:
            break
   return best,best_score
```
If y has all zeros due to x zeros and exp? norm handles.

Potential issue: `np.exp(expr)` each loop allocates; okay.

Now, quick_best_init candidate generation. Use `fft_score` with L. Implementation:
```
def quick_best_init(n, seed=1):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    L=next_pow2(2*n-1)
    cands=[]
    # powers
    for delta in (0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,1.0,2.0): cands.append(make_envelope(n,0.5,delta))
    for tilt in (-0.5,-0.2,0.2,0.5): cands.append(make_envelope(n,0.5,1.0,tilt=tilt))
    for mod_a in (-0.4,-0.2,0.2,0.4): cands.append(make_envelope(n,0.5,1.0,mod_a=mod_a,mod_p=1.0))
    for mod_p in (0.3,0.7,1.5): cands.append(make_envelope(n,0.5,1.0,mod_a=-0.2,mod_p=mod_p))
    for um in (0.1,0.3,0.6): cands.append(make_envelope(n,0.5,1.0,uniform_mix=um))
    for alpha in (0.4,0.6): cands.append(make_envelope(n,alpha,1.0))
    for _ in range(4): cands.append(norm_nonneg(rng.gamma(2.0,size=n)))
    for _ in range(3): cands.append(norm_nonneg(np.exp(-rng.exponential(size=n)/2.0)))
    # reverse of best power?
    for delta in (0.1,1.0):
       x=make_envelope(n,0.5,delta); cands.append(x[::-1])
    best=None; best_sc=np.inf
    for x in cands:
       sc=fft_score(x,n,L)
       if sc<best_sc: best_sc=sc; best=x
    return best if best is not None else norm_nonneg(np.ones(n))
```

Propose_candidate detailed:
```
def propose_candidate(seed=2024, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    t0=time.monotonic()
    deadline=t0+max(0.0,float(budget_s)-30.0)
    best_seq=None; best_fft=np.inf; pool=[]
    # parent quick
    parent=None
    try:
       parent=np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=float)
    except Exception:
       parent=None
    if parent is not None:
       p=norm_nonneg(parent)
       if p.size>0 and np.isfinite(p).all():
          sc=fft_score(p,p.size)
          if sc < best_fft: ...
    ns_alloc = [(256,20),(512,60),(1024,400),(2048,1200),(4096,500)]
    # before LP, quick inits for all? We can inside allocate:
    remaining = deadline - t0
    total_alloc = 2180
    scale = max(0.0, (remaining - 120.0)) / total_alloc
    if scale < 0: scale=0
    for n, base in ns_alloc:
       if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 90: break
       t_actual = int(base * scale)
       if t_actual < 3: continue
       # quick init
       x0 = quick_best_init(n, seed+n)
       sc0 = fft_score(x0,n)
       update pool/best
       # Sometimes if x0 already very good, still run LP
       dl = min(deadline - 90, time.monotonic() + t_actual)
       if dl <= time.monotonic(): continue
       local_best, local_sc = sota_lp_optimize(x0, n, dl, seed+1000+n)
       if local_sc < best_fft: update
       add_candidate(pool, local_best, local_sc)
    # Smooth best if time
    if best_seq is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 40 and len(best_seq) <= 8192:
       # use a smaller slice
       dl_smooth = min(deadline - 20, time.monotonic() + max(20, int(0.05*(deadline-t0))))
       local_best, local_sc = smooth_optimize(best_seq, len(best_seq), dl_smooth, seed+777)
       update
    # If still far and time, maybe run sota on best length with different start? maybe not
    # Final exact
    final_seq=best_seq; final_exact=np.inf
    candidates=[]
    if best_seq is not None: candidates.append((best_fft,best_seq))
    candidates.extend(pool)
    # evaluate best 3 by difficulty? We'll just take first 4 in pool? Pool pruned worst but unsorted.
    # We can select up to 4 with smallest fft by manual nested.
    selected=[]
    while candidates and len(selected)<4:
       min_idx=0; min_val=np.inf
       for i in range(len(candidates)):
          if candidates[i][0] < min_val: min_val=candidates[i][0]; min_idx=i
       selected.append(candidates.pop(min_idx))
    eval_time_limit = deadline - 5
    count=0
    for sc,x in selected:
       if time.monotonic() > eval_time_limit: break
       if x is None or len(x) ==0 or len(x)>10000: continue
       ex=exact_score(x)
       if ex < final_exact:
          final_exact=ex; final_seq=x
       count+=1
    if final_seq is None:
       final_seq = quick_best_init(512, seed)
    return [float(v) for v in np.clip(final_seq,0.0,1000.0)]
```

Now, sota LP memory: `b` length 2n-1 trivial. `res` may allocate. Fine.

This assumes each column length n. For n=2048, 4M writes; okay.

Now, total constraints rows=2n-1. The lower rows have fewer nonzeros but our columns include all; pattern sparse triangular. Good.

```python
"""
Search for non-negative step-function heights minimizing

    E(x) = 2 * n * max_k (x*x)_k / (sum(x))^2

via a combination of:
- an informed initial "endpoint-singular" envelope x_i ~ (i+d)^-1/2,
- a scalable active-set LP direction (the f -> g primal step from the SOTA
  description), implemented as a dense-in-CSC sparse matrix with repeated
  data updates,
- line search transitions x <- (1-t)x + t g,
- fast FFT-based evaluations and optional smooth gradient polishing.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
from scipy.optimize import linprog
from scipy import sparse


def next_pow2(m: int) -> int:
    p = 1
    while p < m:
        p <<= 1
    return p


def norm_nonneg(x) -> np.ndarray:
    """Clip to [0, inf), normalize to sum 1, and return a float64 ndarray."""
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.where(np.isfinite(x), x, 0.0)
    x = np.maximum(x, 0.0)
    s = float(x.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
        n = max(1, x.size)
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=np.float64)
    return x / s


def fft_convolve(x: np.ndarray, n: int, L: int = None) -> np.ndarray:
    """Full linear convolution x*x via FFT, length 2n-1."""
    if L is None:
        L = next_pow2(max(1, 2 * n - 1))
    F = np.fft.rfft(x, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F * F, L)
    return c[: 2 * n - 1]


def fft_score(x: np.ndarray, n: int, L: int = None) -> float:
    """Fast evaluation of the objective using FFT convolution."""
    conv = fft_convolve(x, n, L)
    if conv.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    M = float(np.max(conv))
    if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 0.0:
        return np.inf
    s = float(np.sum(x))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * M / (s * s))


def exact_score(x) -> float:
    """Call the authoritative evaluator, defensively."""
    try:
        out = evaluate_sequence([float(v) for v in x])
        return float(out)
    except Exception:
        return np.inf


def make_envelope(
    n: int,
    alpha: float = 0.5,
    delta: float = 1.0,
    tilt: float = 0.0,
    mod_a: float = 0.0,
    mod_p: float = 1.0,
    uniform_mix: float = 0.0,
    noise: float = 0.0,
    rng=None,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Parametric nonnegative envelope, normalized to sum 1.

    The default alpha=0.5 approximates the continuous extremal shape
    f(t) ~ t^{-1/2}, which already gives a score near pi/2.
    """
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    base = np.power(i + max(float(delta), 1e-6), -float(alpha))

    if tilt != 0.0 and n > 0:
        t = i / float(n)
        base *= 1.0 + float(tilt) * (t - 0.5)

    if mod_a != 0.0 and n > 0:
        t = i / float(n)
        try:
            curve = np.power(t, float(mod_p))
            curve[0] = 0.0
        except Exception:
            curve = t
        base *= 1.0 + float(mod_a) * curve

    if uniform_mix != 0.0:
        base += float(uniform_mix)

    if noise != 0.0 and rng is not None:
        base *= 1.0 + float(noise) * rng.standard_normal(n)

    return norm_nonneg(base)


def quick_best_init(n: int, seed: int = 1) -> np.ndarray:
    """Evaluate a modest bank of informed starts and return the best one."""
    if n <= 0:
        return np.ones(1)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    L = next_pow2(2 * n - 1)

    cands = []

    # Core endpoint-singular shapes.
    for d in (0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0):
        cands.append(make_envelope(n, alpha=0.5, delta=d))

    # Linear tilts: often the best small perturbation from the singular shape.
    for a in (-0.5, -0.25, -0.1, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5):
        cands.append(make_envelope(n, alpha=0.5, delta=1.0, tilt=a))

    # Power-profile modulation.
    for a in (-0.35, -0.2, -0.1, 0.1, 0.2, 0.35):
        cands.append(make_envelope(n, alpha=0.5, delta=1.0, mod_a=a, mod_p=1.0))
    for p in (0.3, 0.7, 1.5):
        cands.append(make_envelope(n, alpha=0.5, delta=1.0, mod_a=-0.2, mod_p=p))

    # Blend with uniform mass.
    for u in (0.1, 0.25, 0.5):
        cands.append(make_envelope(n, alpha=0.5, delta=1.0, uniform_mix=u))

    # Slightly different singular exponents.
    for alpha in (0.45, 0.55):
        cands.append(make_envelope(n, alpha=alpha, delta=1.0))
        cands.append(make_envelope(n, alpha=alpha, delta=0.5))

    # Reversed endpoint-singular sequences.
    for d in (0.1, 1.0):
        x = make_envelope(n, alpha=0.5, delta=d)
        cands.append(x[::-1])

    # A few random nonnegative starts.
    for _ in range(5):
        cands.append(norm_nonneg(rng.gamma(2.0, size=n)))
    for _ in range(3):
        cands.append(norm_nonneg(np.exp(-0.5 * rng.exponential(size=n))))
    for _ in range(2):
        x = make_envelope(
            n,
            alpha=0.5,
            delta=float(rng.uniform(0.05, 2.0)),
            tilt=float(rng.uniform(-0.4, 0.4)),
            mod_a=float(rng.uniform(-0.3, 0.3)),
            noise=0.05,
            rng=rng,
        )
        cands.append(x)

    best = None
    best_sc = np.inf
    for x in cands:
        if x is None or x.size != n or not np.isfinite(x).all():
            continue
        x = norm_nonneg(x)
        sc = fft_score(x, n, L)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = sc
            best = x
    if best is None:
        best = norm_nonneg(make_envelope(n, 0.5, 1.0))
    return best


def build_sota_matrix(n: int):
    """
    Build the sparse matrix A for the LP

        max 1^T g
        s.t. A g <= M_scaled, g >= 0,

    where A has shape (2n-1, n) and column i is f shifted by i.
    Each column has length n.
    """
    if n <= 0:
        raise ValueError("n must be positive")
    rows = 2 * n - 1
    nnz = n * n

    # For safety, avoid absurdly large LP matrices.  This also protects
    # against accidental huge lengths.
    if nnz > 25_000_000:
        raise MemoryError("LP matrix too large")

    indptr = np.arange(n + 1, dtype=np.int32) * np.int32(n)
    indptr[-1] = nnz

    indices = np.empty(nnz, dtype=np.int32)
    for i in range(n):
        st = i * n
        en = st + n
        indices[st:en] = np.arange(i, i + n, dtype=np.int32)

    A = sparse.csc_matrix(
        (np.zeros(nnz, dtype=np.float64), indices, indptr),
        shape=(rows, n),
    )
    return A, indptr, indices


def fill_sota_data(A, f_scaled: np.ndarray, n: int) -> None:
    """
    In-place update of A.data.  Column i receives f_scaled[0:n], so the
    whole matrix data is f_scaled tiled n times.
    """
    d = A.data
    step = n
    for i in range(n):
        st = i * step
        en = st + step
        d[st:en] = f_scaled


def sota_lp_optimize(
    x0,
    n: int,
    deadline: float,
    seed: int = 0,
    verbose: bool = True,
    max_lp_nonzeros: int = 25_000_000,
):
    """
    Run the LP-direction method for a fixed length.

    Returns (best_sequence, best_fft_score).
    """
    x = norm_nonneg(np.asarray(x0, dtype=np.float64))
    n = int(n)
    best = x.copy()
    best_score = fft_score(x, n)

    if n < 2:
        return best, best_score
    if n * n > max_lp_nonzeros:
        if verbose:
            print(f"  SOTA LP skipped: n={n} too large for LP matrix")
        return best, best_score

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    L = next_pow2(2 * n - 1)

    try:
        A, indptr, indices = build_sota_matrix(n)
    except Exception:
        if verbose:
            print(f"  SOTA LP skipped: matrix build failed for n={n}")
        return best, best_score

    base_c = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    rows = 2 * n - 1
    m_factor = 1.0
    stall = 0
    bad = 0
    lp_count = 0
    best_t = 0.25
    step_trials = (1.0, 0.75, 0.5, 1.0 / 3.0, 0.25, 0.125, 0.0625)

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        remaining = max(1.0, float(deadline) - time.monotonic())
        if remaining < 2.0:
            break

        conv = fft_convolve(x, n, L)
        M = float(np.max(conv))
        if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 0.0:
            break

        f_scaled = float(n) * x
        M_scaled = float(n) * M

        # Current score, x is normalized so score = 2 n M.
        current = float(2.0 * n * M)
        if not np.isfinite(current):
            current = fft_score(x, n, L)

        # Update the LP constraint matrix data.
        try:
            fill_sota_data(A, f_scaled, n)
            A_ub = A
        except Exception:
            # Fallback: build a fresh CSC matrix with the same pattern.
            try:
                data = np.empty(A.shape[0] - A.shape[1] + 1 + (A.shape[1] - 1) * 0, dtype=np.float64)
                data = np.empty(int(A.nnz), dtype=np.float64)
                for i in range(n):
                    data[i * n:(i + 1) * n] = f_scaled
                A_ub = sparse.csc_matrix(
                    (data, A.indices.copy(), A.indptr.copy()),
                    shape=A.shape,
                )
            except Exception:
                break

        b_ub = (m_factor * M_scaled) * np.ones(rows, dtype=np.float64)

        # Slight random objective jitter helps escape degenerate LP optima.
        if rng.random() < 0.35:
            c = base_c - 1e-7 * rng.standard_normal(n)
        else:
            c = base_c

        # HiGHS time limit, scaled to the matrix size and remaining budget.
        if n <= 1024:
            tl = 30.0
        elif n <= 2048:
            tl = 90.0
        else:
            tl = 240.0
        tl = min(tl, max(3.0, remaining / 3.0))

        try:
            res = linprog(
                c,
                A_ub=A_ub,
                b_ub=b_ub,
                bounds=(0.0, None),
                method="highs",
                options={"time_limit": float(tl)},
            )
        except Exception:
            res = None

        if res is None or getattr(res, "x", None) is None:
            bad += 1
            m_factor = max(0.7, m_factor * 0.92)
            if bad > 5:
                break
            continue

        if not np.isfinite(res.x).all():
            bad += 1
            if bad > 5:
                break
            continue

        bad = 0
        lp_count += 1

        g = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
        g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)
        sg = float(g.sum())
        if not np.isfinite(sg) or sg <= 1e-12:
            m_factor = max(0.7, m_factor * 0.92)
            stall += 1
            if stall > 12:
                if rng.random() < 0.5:
                    x = norm_nonneg(0.9 * best + 0.1 * rng.standard_normal(n))
                else:
                    x = make_envelope(
                        n,
                        alpha=0.5,
                        delta=float(rng.uniform(0.05, 2.0)),
                        tilt=float(rng.uniform(-0.5, 0.5)),
                        mod_a=float(rng.uniform(-0.35, 0.35)),
                        noise=0.05,
                        rng=rng,
                    )
                current = fft_score(x, n, L)
                stall = 0
                m_factor = 1.0
            continue

        g /= sg

        # Line search over relaxation coefficient t.
        best_y = None
        best_y_score = current
        best_t_used = best_t
        for t in step_trials:
            y = (1.0 - float(t)) * x + float(t) * g
            y = norm_nonneg(y)
            sc = fft_score(y, n, L)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_y_score:
                best_y_score = sc
                best_y = y
                best_t_used = float(t)

        if best_y is not None:
            x = best_y
            current = best_y_score
            best_t = best_t_used

            if current < best_score:
                best_score = current
                best = x.copy()
                stall = 0
                m_factor = 1.0
                if verbose:
                    print(
                        f"  SOTA n={n} iter={lp_count} base_lt_score={best_score:.7f} t={best_t_used:.4f}",
                        flush=True,
                    )
        else:
            stall += 1
            if stall in (3, 6, 12, 20, 30):
                m_factor = max(0.70, m_factor * 0.94)

            if stall > 25:
                # Restart the local iteration, but never destroy the best found.
                if rng.random() < 0.45:
                    x = norm_nonneg(0.92 * best + 0.08 * rng.standard_normal(n))
                else:
                    x = make_envelope(
                        n,
                        alpha=0.5,
                        delta=float(rng.uniform(0.05, 2.0)),
                        tilt=float(rng.uniform(-0.6, 0.6)),
                        mod_a=float(rng.uniform(-0.4, 0.4)),
                        mod_p=float(rng.uniform(0.4, 1.8)),
                        noise=0.06,
                        rng=rng,
                    )
                current = fft_score(x, n, L)
                stall = 0
                m_factor = 1.0

    return best, best_score


def smooth_optimize(x0, n: int, deadline: float, seed: int = 0, verbose: bool = False):
    """
    Optional fast FFT smooth-max gradient polish.
    Not the main driver, but can squeeze a little extra precision.
    """
    x = norm_nonneg(np.asarray(x0, dtype=np.float64))
    n = int(n)
    best = x.copy()
    best_score = fft_score(x, n)

    if n < 2 or time.monotonic() >= deadline:
        return best, best_score

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    L1 = next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    L2 = next_pow2(3 * n - 2)

    tau = 5.0
    eta = 0.2
    stall = 0
    bad = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        remaining = max(1.0, float(deadline) - time.monotonic())
        if remaining < 2.0:
            break

        conv = fft_convolve(x, n, L1)
        M = float(np.max(conv))
        if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 0.0:
            break

        shifted = tau * (conv - M) / M
        np.clip(shifted, -700.0, 0.0, out=shifted)
        w = np.exp(shifted)
        ws = float(w.sum())
        if not np.isfinite(ws) or ws <= 0.0:
            break
        w /= ws

        # grad of smooth max: 2 * sum_k w[k] x[k-i]
        W = np.fft.rfft(w, L2)
        X = np.fft.rfft(x, L2)
        G = np.fft.irfft(W * np.conj(X), L2)[:n]
        grad = 2.0 * G

        # Remove the component that changes the sum; we will renormalize anyway.
        grad -= float(np.mean(grad))
        scale = float(np.max(np.abs(grad)))
        if not np.isfinite(scale) or scale <= 1e-300:
            break

        gn = grad / scale
        best_y = None
        best_sc = float(np.min((best_score, fft_score(x, n, L1))))

        for rel in (eta, eta * 0.5, eta * 0.25, 0.05, 0.01):
            expr = -float(rel) * gn
            np.clip(expr, -4.0, 4.0, out=expr)
            y = x * np.exp(expr)
            y = norm_nonneg(y)
            sc = fft_score(y, n, L1)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
                best_sc = sc
                best_y = y

        if best_y is not None:
            x = best_y
            if best_sc < best_score:
                best_score = best_sc
                best = x.copy()
            stall = 0
            bad = 0
            tau = min(250.0, tau * 1.04)
            eta = min(1.0, eta * 1.05)
            if verbose and best_score < 1.6:
                print(f"  smooth n={n} score={best_score:.7f}", flush=True)
        else:
            stall += 1
            bad += 1
            if stall > 10:
                x = norm_nonneg(0.92 * best + 0.08 * rng.standard_normal(n))
                tau = max(2.0, tau * 0.85)
                eta = max(0.03, eta * 0.7)
                stall = 0
                bad = 0
            if bad > 35:
                break

    return best, best_score


def add_candidate(pool, x, score, max_items: int = 8):
    """Keep a small pool of good candidates for final exact evaluation."""
    if x is None or not np.isfinite(score):
        return
    pool.append((float(score), np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).copy()))
    while len(pool) > max_items:
        worst_idx = 0
        worst_val = -np.inf
        for i in range(len(pool)):
            if pool[i][0] > worst_val:
                worst_val = pool[i][0]
                worst_idx = i
        pool.pop(worst_idx)


def propose_candidate(seed: int = 12345, budget_s: float = 2400.0, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 35.0)

    best_seq = None
    best_fft = np.inf
    pool = []

    # If a previous construction is available, consider it, but do not rely on it.
    try:
        parent = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    if parent is not None and parent.size > 0:
        p = norm_nonneg(parent)
        if np.isfinite(p).all():
            sc = fft_score(p, p.size)
            if sc < best_fft:
                best_fft = sc
                best_seq = p
                add_candidate(pool, p, sc)

    # Primary search by LP-direction method on several lengths.
    # 2048 is a good balance between optimality headroom and LP cost.
    base_alloc = [
        (256, 25),
        (512, 75),
        (1024, 470),
        (2048, 1230),
        (4096, 420),
    ]
    total_alloc = sum(t for _, t in base_alloc)
    allocation_budget = max(0.0, (deadline - t0) - 180.0)
    scale = allocation_budget / float(total_alloc) if total_alloc > 0 else 0.0

    for n, base_t in base_alloc:
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 120.0:
            break
        t_actual = int(base_t * max(0.0, scale))
        if t_actual < 4:
            continue

        slice_deadline = min(deadline - 120.0, time.monotonic() + float(t_actual))
        if slice_deadline <= time.monotonic():
            continue

        x0 = quick_best_init(n, seed + n)
        sc0 = fft_score(x0, n)
        if sc0 < best_fft:
            best_fft = sc0
            best_seq = x0
            add_candidate(pool, x0, sc0)

        if verbose := True:
            print(f"\nSOTA-LP n={n}, init_score={sc0:.7f}, budget={t_actual}s", flush=True)

        local_best, local_sc = sota_lp_optimize(x0, n, slice_deadline, seed + 1000 + n)
        if np.isfinite(local_sc):
            add_candidate(pool, local_best, local_sc)
            if local_sc < best_fft:
                best_fft = local_sc
                best_seq = local_best
                print(f"  global best updated to {best_fft:.7f} at n={n}", flush=True)

    # Optional fast polish from the current best sequence.
    if best_seq is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 45.0:
        m = int(best_seq.size)
        if 2 <= m <= 8192:
            smooth_deadline = min(deadline - 25.0, time.monotonic() + 90.0)
            if smooth_deadline > time.monotonic() + 5.0:
                pol, pol_sc = smooth_optimize(best_seq, m, smooth_deadline, seed + 777)
                if np.isfinite(pol_sc) and pol_sc < best_fft:
                    best_fft = pol_sc
                    best_seq = pol
                    add_candidate(pool, pol, pol_sc)
                    print(f"  smooth polish best={best_fft:.7f}", flush=True)

    # One more small safe retry if the result is still far from target and time remains.
    if (best_seq is None or best_fft > 1.55) and time.monotonic() < deadline - 120.0:
        small_n = 1024 if best_seq is None or best_seq.size > 4096 else max(512, min(2048, best_seq.size))
        extra_deadline = min(deadline - 40.0, time.monotonic() + 150.0)
        if extra_deadline > time.monotonic() + 5.0:
            x0 = quick_best_init(small_n, seed + 9000)
            local_best, local_sc = sota_lp_optimize(x0, small_n, extra_deadline, seed + 9100)
            if np.isfinite(local_sc) and local_sc < best_fft:
                best_fft = local_sc
                best_seq = local_best
                add_candidate(pool, local_best, local_sc)

    # Final authoritative evaluation of a few candidates.  Cap this to avoid
    # spending the whole run on O(n^2) evaluation.
    candidates = []
    if best_seq is not None:
        candidates.append((best_fft, best_seq))
    candidates.extend(pool)

    # Choose at most four lowest-FFT candidates.
    selected = []
    while candidates and len(selected) < 4:
        min_idx = 0
        min_val = np.inf
        for i in range(len(candidates)):
            if candidates[i][0] < min_val:
                min_val = candidates[i][0]
                min_idx = i
        selected.append(candidates.pop(min_idx))

    final_seq = best_seq
    final_exact = np.inf
    exact_deadline = deadline - 3.0
    exact_count = 0
    seen_lens = set()

    for _, x in selected:
        if time.monotonic() > exact_deadline:
            break
        if x is None or len(x) == 0 or len(x) > 12000:
            continue
        if len(x) in seen_lens:
            # Different sequences of same length may exist, but exact is expensive.
            # Evaluate at most two per length.
            exact_count += 1
            if exact_count > 5:
                break
        ex = exact_score(x)
        if np.isfinite(ex):
            exact_count += 1
            seen_lens.add(len(x))
            if ex < final_exact:
                final_exact = ex
                final_seq = x
        if exact_count >= 6:
            break

    if final_seq is None:
        final_seq = quick_best_init(1024, seed + 555)

    final_seq = norm_nonneg(final_seq)
    final_seq = np.clip(final_seq, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return [float(v) for v in final_seq]
```
